Multi-Cluster
EU+US mesh
🪽
Ambient
No sidecar

💼 Part 8 — Daily Work Takeaways

  • Run full canary: deploy v2 → 5% → 25% → 50% → 100% over days
  • Rollback instantly: change weight to 0% — one YAML edit, no downtime
  • Use traffic mirroring to test v2 with REAL production traffic safely
  • Do blue-green: instant 100% switch, instant rollback
  • Add local rate limiting per pod (EnvoyFilter) to protect Django
  • Understand multi-cluster: EU + US with locality failover
  • Add legacy VMs via WorkloadEntry for full mesh integration
  • Evaluate ambient mesh: 6× less memory, no sidecars

🚀 Part 9: Production Operations — Running Istio at Scale Chapter 17

Chapter 17: Running Istio in Production

The final chapter — everything you need to upgrade, scale, troubleshoot, and operate Istio in production. From canary upgrades to HA istiod, from resource sizing to the go-live checklist.

🔴 Advanced ⏱️ ~2 hours 🏭 Production 🎯 ICA 15%
🎓 ICA Relevance
Troubleshooting 15% Operations ALL DOMAINS
💡 ICA Exam Tips for Chapter 17 (Troubleshooting — 15%)
  • "Why isn't VirtualService routing?" = Check DestinationRule subsets match pod labels. Run istioctl analyze -n <ns>
  • "Why is mTLS failing?" = istioctl authn tls-check <pod>
  • "Why isn't sidecar injected?" = Check namespace label: kubectl get ns <ns> --show-labels
  • "How to upgrade Istio?" = Canary upgrade: old+new istiod in parallel, migrate namespaces one by one

17.1 Upgrading Istio — Safely in Production

Istio releases every ~3 months. NEVER use istioctl upgrade (in-place) in production. Use canary upgrade — run old and new istiod simultaneously.

🔄 CANARY ISTIO UPGRADE — Old + New istiod in Parallel
1
Install New istiod

istioctl install --set revision=1-22
Both old + new run side-by-side

2
Label Test NS

kubectl label ns anihpj-test istio.io/rev=1-22
Test namespace uses new istiod

3
Validate

Check Kiali, Grafana, proxy-status
All SYNCED with new istiod? ✅

4
Migrate Prod NS

Label anihpj → istio.io/rev=1-22
Rolling restart pods → new sidecars

5
Remove Old

All namespaces migrated → delete old istiod
✅ Upgrade complete, zero downtime!

17.2 Performance Tuning and Resource Sizing

📊 RESOURCE SIZING TABLE — Plan Your Cluster

ScalePodsistiodSidecarsTotal Extra
Dev (anihpj)~101 rep / 500m 2Gi10×128Mi + 10×100m~2Gi RAM / ~1.5 CPU
Small Prod~502 reps / 1c 4Gi50×128Mi + 50×100m~8Gi RAM / ~6 CPU
Medium Prod~1003 reps / 1.5c 6Gi100×128Mi + 100×100m~15Gi RAM / ~11.5 CPU
Large Prod~5003+ reps / 3c 12Gi500×128Mi + 500×100m~67Gi RAM / ~53 CPU
💡 Performance Quick Wins

Reduce tracing sampling from 100% (dev) to 1% (prod) — huge CPU savings. Use HTTP/2 for gRPC services. Consider ambient mesh for 6× less memory overhead.

17.3 High Availability for istiod

Run 3 istiod replicas across availability zones with PodDisruptionBudget. If istiod goes down: existing proxies KEEP working with cached config.

🏗️ istiod HA — 3 Replicas Across Zones
Zone A

istiod replica 1
Node 1 → sidecars

Zone B

istiod replica 2
Node 2 → sidecars

Zone C

istiod replica 3
Node 3 → sidecars

bash — Scale istiod for HA 

# Scale istiod to 3 replicas
kubectl patch deployment istiod -n istio-system \
  -p '{"spec":{"replicas":3}}'

# Apply PodDisruptionBudget (min 2 available)
kubectl create pdb istiod-pdb -n istio-system \
  --selector=app=istiod --min-available=2

# If Zone A fails: replicas 2+3 take over
# Existing proxies: KEEP WORKING with cached config ✅

bash — Essential Troubleshooting Commands 

# Check proxy sync (all should be SYNCED)

# Validate configuration (catches 80% of errors!)

# Debug mTLS issues
istioctl authn tls-check deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj

# Inspect Envoy config

istioctl proxy-config route deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj

# Check certificates
istioctl proxy-config secret deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj

→ Check sampling rate (100%?)

📝 Chapter 17 — ICA Practice Questions

Q1
What is the FIRST command to run when Istio isn't working?
Show Answer
Answer
istioctl analyze -n anihpj

Catches ~80% of configuration errors before deep debugging. Run it BEFORE checking logs, restarting pods, or inspecting proxies.

Explanation

istioctl analyze is your first troubleshooting step — always. It validates all Istio CRDs in the namespace and catches: missing subsets, port naming issues, Gateway-VirtualService mismatches, mTLS config errors, and conflicting rules. On the ICA exam, this command is the first thing you run for any Istio problem.

Q2
How do you check if a specific pod has valid mTLS configuration?
Show Answer
Answer
istioctl authn tls-check deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj

Shows: certificate validity period, trust chain (root CA → workload cert), mTLS status per port, and whether PeerAuthentication is being enforced.

Explanation

This command is essential for mTLS debugging. It shows you the exact certificate being used by Envoy, who issued it, when it expires, and whether mTLS is active. On the ICA exam, when troubleshooting 503 errors between services, this command verifies mTLS is configured correctly on both sides.

Q3
What's the safest way to upgrade Istio in production, and why NOT use istioctl upgrade?
Show Answer
Answer

Canary upgrade: Install new istiod alongside old (revision labels), migrate namespaces one by one (istio.io/rev=1-22), rolling restart pods, then remove old istiod. NEVER use istioctl upgrade — it replaces istiod in-place with no rollback path.

Explanation

Canary upgrade is the production-safe approach: old and new istiod run simultaneously. If the new version has issues, you simply remove the revision label from affected namespaces — instant rollback. On the ICA exam, canary upgrade = production standard.

Q4
What happens to existing traffic and proxies if istiod goes down?
Show Answer
Answer

Existing proxies KEEP WORKING with cached configuration. Traffic continues flowing. Only NEW pods (new sidecars) and configuration CHANGES are affected — new pods can't start, CRD changes won't propagate. Run 3 istiod replicas + PDB (minAvailable: 2) for HA.

Explanation

This is the architectural resilience of separating control and data planes. The data plane (Envoy) operates independently with last-known-good config. On the ICA exam, this is a key concept: "control plane failure does NOT cause data plane traffic interruption."

Q5
How much resources does each sidecar add per pod, and what's the total for anihpj (7 services)?
Show Answer
Answer

~128Mi RAM + ~100m CPU per sidecar. anihpj (7 services): ~900Mi RAM + ~700m CPU total overhead. Ambient mesh alternative: ~50Mi total (one ztunnel per node), 6× less memory.

Explanation

Resource planning is critical for production. Each sidecar is a full Envoy proxy — it needs dedicated resources. Plan for: Dev (10 pods) = ~2Gi, Small Prod (50 pods) = ~8Gi, Medium Prod (100 pods) = ~15Gi. On the ICA exam, know the ~128Mi per sidecar rule of thumb.

Q6
What are the top 3 reasons VirtualService routing fails?
Show Answer
Answer

(1) DR subsets don't match pod labels — most common cause of 503s. (2) Port names don't start with protocol prefix (http/grpc/tcp/tls) — Istio treats as plain TCP. (3) VS hosts don't match Service name or Gateway hosts — traffic silently dropped.

Explanation

All three are caught by istioctl analyze. On the ICA exam, when troubleshooting routing issues, check these three things in order: subset labels → port names → host matching. This covers ~80% of routing failures.

Q7
How do you integrate Istio configuration validation into CI/CD pipeline?
Show Answer
Answer
# In .github/workflows or Jenkinsfile:
istioctl analyze -f istio/ --failure-threshold WARNING
# Block PR if analysis finds errors

Store Istio CRDs alongside app manifests in Git. Use ArgoCD/Flux for GitOps — sync Gateway, VS, DR, PeerAuthentication, AuthorizationPolicy with application deployments.

Explanation

Shift-left: catch Istio config errors before they reach the cluster. Run istioctl analyze as a CI check. On the ICA exam, integrating Istio validation into CI/CD is a best practice for production operations.

📝 Part 9 Summary — Production Operations (15% of ICA)

  • Upgrade (17.1): Canary upgrade — old+new istiod in parallel. Migrate namespaces one by one.
  • Performance (17.2): Each sidecar = ~128Mi RAM. Plan cluster resources accordingly.
  • HA (17.3): 3 istiod replicas across zones + PDB. Existing proxies survive istiod failure.
  • Troubleshooting (17.4): ALWAYS start with istioctl analyze. Know the top 5 problems + fixes.
  • CI/CD (17.5): Shift-left: validate Istio config in PR before it hits production.

🎯 ICA Certification Master Prep

📋 ICA Exam Format

Duration: 2 hours | Format: Hands-on CLI tasks in a live Istio + Kubernetes environment | Open Book: Yes — you can access istio.io/docs during the exam | Passing Score: ~75%

🔑 TOP 20 MUST-KNOW COMMANDS & FACTS FOR ICA
1. Install Istio

istioctl install --set profile=demo -y

2. Enable Injection

kubectl label ns X istio-injection=enabled

3. Check Injection

kubectl get pods → MUST show 2/2

4. Validate Config

istioctl analyze -n <ns> — catches 80% of errors!

5. Check Sync

istioctl proxy-status → all SYNCED

6. Gateway + VS Rule

Gateway WITHOUT VirtualService = traffic DROPPED

7. Port Names

MUST be "http"/"grpc"/"tcp"/"tls" — Istio uses port NAME not number

8. Canary Pattern

VS: weight v1=90, v2=10 + DR: subsets v1/v2

9. mTLS STRICT

PeerAuthentication: spec.mtls.mode: STRICT

10. Deny All

AuthorizationPolicy: spec: {} = deny everything

11. Circuit Breaker

DR outlierDetection.consecutiveGatewayErrors: 5

12. Retries

VS retries: {attempts: 3, perTryTimeout: 2s} — idempotent only!

13. External APIs

ServiceEntry: location: MESH_EXTERNAL, resolution: DNS

14. Mirror Traffic

VS mirror: {host: X, subset: v2} — v2 gets silent copy

15. Pod Labels

app: X AND version: v1 — REQUIRED for subsets!

16. Debug mTLS

istioctl authn tls-check <pod> -n <ns>

17. Inspect Envoy

istioctl proxy-config cluster <pod> -n <ns>

18. Open Dashboards

istioctl dashboard kiali / grafana / jaeger

19. Sidecar NOT injected?

Namespace label missing OR pod existed before labeling

20. VS not routing?

DR subsets don't match pod labels → 503 errors

⚠️ TOP 10 ICA EXAM MISTAKES — Avoid These!
#MistakeWhy It Costs PointsCorrect Approach
1Forgetting port name prefixIstio treats as TCP, all L7 features disabledAlways use "http"/"grpc"/"tcp"/"tls"
2Gateway without VirtualServiceTraffic accepted but DROPPED — 404Always pair GW with VS via gateways field
3Missing version label on podsDR subsets resolve to ZERO endpoints → 503Add labels: {app: X, version: v1}
4Not running istioctl analyzeConfig errors go undetectedALWAYS run analyze before considering done
5Using istioctl upgrade in prodIn-place upgrade risks downtimeAlways canary upgrade: old+new istiod
6Not forwarding tracing headersBroken traces — isolated spans onlyPropagate x-b3-* headers in all HTTP calls
7mTLS without AuthzPolicyAny service can call any servicemTLS = identity, AuthzPolicy = permissions
8Retries on non-idempotent requestsDouble-charge payments, duplicate recordsOnly retry GET; use retryOn: 5xx only
9retries × perTryTimeout > timeoutTimeout fires before retries completeAlways: retries × perTryTimeout < timeout
10REGISTRY_ONLY without ServiceEntriesAll external APIs blocked with 502Create ServiceEntry BEFORE switching mode

☐ Set kubectl alias: alias k=kubectl
☐ Set namespace: k config set-context --current --namespace=<ns>
☐ Verify cluster: k get nodes

For Every Task

☐ Run istioctl analyze -n <ns> after changes
☐ Check k get pods → 2/2
☐ Verify with curl before moving on

Time Management

☐ 2h = 120min ÷ tasks ≈ 7-8min each

📎 Appendix A: Istio CRD Quick Reference

CRDAPI GroupPurpose
VirtualServicenetworking.istio.ioTraffic routing rules (URI, headers, weight)
DestinationRulenetworking.istio.ioPolicies: LB, subsets, circuit breaker, mTLS
Gatewaynetworking.istio.ioMesh ingress/egress entry point
ServiceEntrynetworking.istio.ioRegister external services in mesh
EnvoyFilternetworking.istio.ioLow-level Envoy config override
WorkloadEntrynetworking.istio.ioRegister VM/external workload

📎 Appendix B: Complete anihpj Istio YAML Manifests

B.1 Namespace Setup

yaml — Namespace with Istio injection enabled 

apiVersion: v1
kind: Namespace
metadata:
  name: anihpj
  labels:
    istio-injection: enabled    # ← KEY: enables auto sidecar injection
    environment: production
---
# Verify:
# kubectl get namespace anihpj --show-labels
# NAME      STATUS   LABELS
# anihpj    Active   istio-injection=enabled,environment=prod

B.2 Dockerfile for anihpj/jobpost-api

dockerfile — anihpj/jobpost/Dockerfile 

FROM python:3.11-slim
WORKDIR /app

# Install dependencies (Docker layer cache optimization)
COPY requirements.txt .
RUN pip install --no-cache-dir -r requirements.txt

# Copy application code
COPY . .
RUN python manage.py collectstatic --noinput

# Health check (required for K8s liveness probe)
HEALTHCHECK --interval=30s --timeout=10s --retries=3 \
  CMD curl -f http://localhost:8000/health/ || exit 1

EXPOSE 8000
CMD ["gunicorn", "--bind", "0.0.0.0:8000",
     "--workers", "4", "--timeout", "120",
     "anihpj.wsgi:application"]

# Build: docker build -t anihpj/jobpost-api:v1 .
# Push:  docker push anihpj/jobpost-api:v1

B.3 Complete Deployment + Service

yaml — Deployment + Service for jobpost-api 

apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: jobpost-api
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  replicas: 3
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: jobpost-api
      version: v1
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app: jobpost-api      # ← REQUIRED for Istio/Kiali
        version: v1           # ← REQUIRED for canary subsets
    spec:
      containers:
      - name: jobpost
        image: anihpj/jobpost-api:v1
        ports:
        - name: http          # ← REQUIRED: Istio uses port name
          containerPort: 8000
        readinessProbe:
          httpGet:
            path: /health/
            port: 8000
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: jobpost-api           # ← VirtualService references this
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  selector:
    app: jobpost-api          # ← matches Deployment label
  ports:
  - name: http                # ← MUST be "http"
    port: 80
    targetPort: 8000

B.4-B.13 — Gateway, VirtualService, DestinationRule, PeerAuthentication, AuthorizationPolicy, ServiceEntry, RequestAuthentication, and Telemetry are covered in detail within their respective chapters.

B.4 Istio Gateway for anihpj.example.com

yaml — anihpj-gateway.yaml 

apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1alpha3
kind: Gateway
metadata:
  name: anihpj-gateway
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  selector:
    istio: ingressgateway
  servers:
  - port:
      number: 80
      name: http
      protocol: HTTP
    hosts:
    - "anihpj.example.com"
  - port:
      number: 443
      name: https
      protocol: HTTPS
    tls:
      mode: SIMPLE
      credentialName: anihpj-tls-cert
    hosts:
    - "anihpj.example.com"

B.5 VirtualService — Path-Based Routing

yaml — path-based routing for all services 

apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1beta1
kind: VirtualService
metadata:
  name: anihpj-routes
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  gateways:
  - anihpj-gateway
  hosts:
  - "anihpj.example.com"
  http:
  - match:
    - uri:
        prefix: /jobs/
    route:
    - destination:
        host: jobpost-api
  - match:
    - uri:
        prefix: /users/
    route:
    - destination:
        host: user-service
  - match:
    - uri:
        prefix: /skills/
    route:
    - destination:
        host: skills-service
  - route:
    - destination:
        host: jobpost-api

B.6 VirtualService — Canary (90/10 Split)

yaml — canary deployment for jobpost-api 

apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1beta1
kind: VirtualService
metadata:
  name: jobpost-api-vs
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  hosts:
  - jobpost-api
  http:
  - route:
    - destination:
        host: jobpost-api
        subset: v1
      weight: 90
    - destination:
        host: jobpost-api
        subset: v2
      weight: 10

B.7 DestinationRule — Subsets, LEAST_CONN, Circuit Breaker

yaml — DestinationRule for jobpost-api 

apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1alpha3
kind: DestinationRule
metadata:
  name: jobpost-api-dr
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  host: jobpost-api
  trafficPolicy:
    loadBalancer:
      simple: LEAST_CONN
    connectionPool:
      tcp:
        maxConnections: 100
      http:
        http1MaxPendingRequests: 50
    outlierDetection:
      consecutiveGatewayErrors: 5
      interval: 30s
      baseEjectionTime: 30s
      maxEjectionPercent: 50
    tls:
      mode: ISTIO_MUTUAL
  subsets:
  - name: v1
    labels:
      version: v1
  - name: v2
    labels:
      version: v2

B.8 PeerAuthentication — STRICT mTLS

yaml — PeerAuthentication for anihpj 

apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1beta1
kind: PeerAuthentication
metadata:
  name: default
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  mtls:
    mode: STRICT

B.9 AuthorizationPolicy — Deny-All Baseline

yaml — deny-all policy 

apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1beta1
kind: AuthorizationPolicy
metadata:
  name: deny-all
  namespace: anihpj
spec: {}

B.10 AuthorizationPolicy — Allow Rules

yaml — allow ingress → jobpost + jobpost → users 

# Allow ingress → jobpost-api (GET/POST)
apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1beta1
kind: AuthorizationPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-ingress-to-jobpost
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: jobpost-api
  action: ALLOW
  rules:
  - from:
    - source:
        principals:
        - "cluster.local/ns/istio-system/sa/istio-ingressgateway"
    to:
    - operation:
        methods: ["GET", "POST"]
---
# Allow jobpost-api → user-service (GET only)
apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1beta1
kind: AuthorizationPolicy
metadata:
  name: allow-jobpost-to-users
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app: user-service
  action: ALLOW
  rules:
  - from:
    - source:
        principals:
        - "cluster.local/ns/anihpj/sa/jobpost-api"
    to:
    - operation:
        methods: ["GET"]
        paths: ["/api/users/*"]

B.11 ServiceEntry — External Payment API

yaml — ServiceEntry for Stripe 

apiVersion: networking.istio.io/v1alpha3
kind: ServiceEntry
metadata:
  name: stripe-payments
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  hosts:
  - api.stripe.com
  ports:
  - number: 443
    name: https
    protocol: HTTPS
  location: MESH_EXTERNAL
  resolution: DNS

B.12 RequestAuthentication — JWT Validation

yaml — JWT validation for API endpoints 

apiVersion: security.istio.io/v1beta1
kind: RequestAuthentication
metadata:
  name: jwt-auth
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  jwtRules:
  - issuer: "auth-service.anihpj.svc.cluster.local"
    jwksUri: "http://auth-service/.well-known/jwks.json"

B.13 Telemetry — Custom Sampling + Metrics Labels

yaml — Telemetry resource 

apiVersion: telemetry.istio.io/v1alpha1
kind: Telemetry
metadata:
  name: anihpj-telemetry
  namespace: anihpj
spec:
  tracing:
  - randomSamplingPercentage: 1.0
  metrics:
  - providers:
    - name: prometheus
    overrides:
    - match:
        metric: REQUEST_COUNT
      tagOverrides:
        job_category:
          value: request.headers["x-job-category"]

🔍 DEBUGGING & DIAGNOSTICS

CommandPurpose
istioctl proxy-statusCheck sync status of ALL proxies (SYNCED/STALE)
istioctl proxy-config cluster <pod> -n <ns>Show upstream clusters Envoy knows about
istioctl proxy-config route <pod> -n <ns>Show routing rules applied to this proxy
istioctl proxy-config listener <pod> -n <ns>Show listeners (ports) on this proxy
istioctl proxy-config endpoint <pod> -n <ns>Show endpoint discovery (which pods exist)
istioctl proxy-config secret <pod> -n <ns>Show SPIFFE certificates in Envoy
istioctl analyze -n <ns>Validate config — catches 80% of errors!
istioctl analyze --all-namespacesValidate config across entire cluster
istioctl authn tls-check <pod> -n <ns>Check mTLS configuration for a pod
istioctl bug-reportGenerate diagnostic bundle for support

🖥️ DASHBOARDS

CommandPurpose
istioctl dashboard kialiOpen Kiali — service graph, config validation
istioctl dashboard grafanaOpen Grafana — RED dashboards
istioctl dashboard jaegerOpen Jaeger — distributed traces
istioctl dashboard prometheusOpen Prometheus — raw metrics query
istioctl dashboard zipkinOpen Zipkin — alternative tracing backend
istioctl dashboard envoy <pod> -n <ns>Open Envoy admin UI for a specific proxy
💡 ICA Exam Tip

The 3 most important istioctl commands for the exam: istioctl analyze -n <ns> (validate config), istioctl proxy-status (check sync), istioctl proxy-config cluster <pod> -n <ns> (inspect Envoy). Know these by heart.

📎 Appendix D: Common Istio Errors & Solutions

Error / SymptomLikely CauseFix
Pod shows 1/1 not 2/2Sidecar not injectedLabel ns: istio-injection=enabled
503 RBAC: access deniedAuthorizationPolicy blockingAdd explicit ALLOW policy
503 upstream connect errorDR mTLS mode mismatchMatch PeerAuthentication mode
VS not routingSubset not in DestinationRuleAdd matching subset to DR
Traffic to all podsMissing version labelAdd version label to Deployment
analyze: "host not found"Service name mismatch in VSMatch VS host to K8s svc name
High latency after installTracing 100% sampling overheadSet sampling to 1% in Telemetry
Circuit breaker trips oftenoutlierDetection too aggressiveIncrease consecutiveGatewayErrors
External APIs failingREGISTRY_ONLY mode activeAdd ServiceEntry for external host
PROXY_STATUS: STALENetwork issue to istiodCheck istiod pod health, port 15012

📎 Appendix E: Istio Terminology Glossary

TermDefinition
Ambient MeshSidecar-free Istio mode using ztunnel (L4) + waypoint proxies (L7)
AuthorizationPolicyIstio CRD for access control — defines WHO can call WHAT
Circuit BreakerPattern: stop sending requests to a failing service to prevent cascading failures
Control Planeistiod — manages configuration, certificates, and policies for all proxies
CRDCustom Resource Definition — extends Kubernetes API with Istio resources
Data PlaneAll Envoy sidecar proxies that handle actual service-to-service traffic
DestinationRuleDefines policies for traffic going TO a service: LB, subsets, circuit breaker, mTLS
EnvoyHigh-performance L7 proxy; Istio's sidecar proxy handling all mesh traffic
Fault InjectionDeliberately inject delays/errors to test system resilience
Gateway (Istio)Entry/exit point to the mesh for external traffic (ingress + egress)
istiodIstio control plane daemon (Pilot + Citadel + Galley combined)
istioctlIstio CLI tool — like kubectl but for Istio operations
JaegerDistributed tracing backend; visualizes request traces as Gantt charts
KialiIstio mesh visualization console — service graph, validation, YAML wizards
mTLSMutual TLS — both client and server verify each other's certificates
Outlier DetectionDestinationRule setting that implements circuit breaking by ejecting failing hosts
PeerAuthenticationIstio CRD that configures mTLS mode (PERMISSIVE/STRICT/DISABLE)
Pilotistiod component: reads CRDs, translates to Envoy config via xDS API
RED MetricsRate, Errors, Duration — Istio auto-collects these from every Envoy proxy
RequestAuthenticationIstio CRD for JWT token validation at the mesh level
Service MeshInfrastructure layer that manages service-to-service communication
ServiceEntryIstio CRD to register external services (Stripe, SendGrid, S3) in the mesh
Sidecar (container)Envoy proxy container injected alongside your application container
SPIFFESecure Production Identity Framework — certificate standard used by Istio
SVIDSPIFFE Verifiable Identity Document — workload X.509 certificate
TelemetryIstio CRD for configuring metrics, tracing, and logging collection
VirtualServiceIstio CRD defining routing rules: URI match, weight split, retries, timeouts
Waypoint ProxyAmbient mesh L7 proxy per namespace (optional, replaces sidecar L7 features)
xDS APIProtocol used by istiod to dynamically configure all Envoy proxies
ztunnelAmbient mesh L4 proxy per Node; handles mTLS without per-pod sidecars

📎 Appendix G: Local Dev Quick Start

bash — From zero to Istio mesh in 10 commands 

# 1. Start minikube (4 CPUs, 8GB RAM, 40GB disk)
minikube start --cpus=4 --memory=8192 --disk-size=40g

# 2. Install Istio (demo profile = all tools)
curl -L https://istio.io/downloadIstio | sh -
cd istio-1.22.*
export PATH=$PWD/bin:$PATH
istioctl install --set profile=demo -y

# 3. Verify installation (7 pods running)
kubectl get pods -n istio-system

# 4. Create and label anihpj namespace
kubectl create namespace anihpj
kubectl label namespace anihpj istio-injection=enabled

# 5. Deploy anihpj
kubectl apply -f anihpj/k8s/ -n anihpj

# 6. Verify sidecars (all pods should show 2/2)
kubectl get pods -n anihpj

# 7. Check proxy sync (all should be SYNCED)
istioctl proxy-status

# 8. Validate config (0 errors expected)
istioctl analyze -n anihpj

# 9. Open dashboards
istioctl dashboard kiali
istioctl dashboard grafana
istioctl dashboard jaeger

# 10. Test traffic
kubectl exec -it deploy/jobpost-api -n anihpj -- \
  curl -s user-service/users/

📎 Appendix H: Quick-Reference Cheat Sheet

🧭 ISTIO CRD SELECTION GUIDE

I want to...Use this Istio resource
Accept external HTTPS trafficGateway
Route by URL pathVirtualService (http.match.uri)
Do a canary deploymentVirtualService (weight) + DestinationRule (subsets)
Add retry logic / timeoutVirtualService (retries / timeout)
Inject faults for testingVirtualService (fault)
Load balance / circuit breakerDestinationRule (loadBalancer / outlierDetection)
Encrypt service-to-servicePeerAuthentication (STRICT)
Block a service from callingAuthorizationPolicy (DENY)
Validate JWT tokensRequestAuthentication
Call an external APIServiceEntry
Add non-K8s workloads to meshWorkloadEntry
See service topology / traces / metricsKiali / Jaeger / Grafana
🔑
mTLS Modes

PERMISSIVE — allow both HTTP + mTLS (migration)
STRICT — mTLS only
DISABLE — no TLS

⚖️
Canary Pattern

1. Deploy v2 (replicas: 2)
2. VS weight: v1=95%, v2=5%
3. Monitor Kiali/Grafana
4. Ramp: 10→25→50→100%
Rollback: set v2=0% instantly

📊
RED Metrics (auto)

R = Rate (req/s)
E = Errors (% 4xx/5xx)
D = Duration (p50/p95/p99)
Zero code changes!

🔌
Circuit Breaker

outlierDetection:
  consecutiveGatewayErrors: 5
  interval: 30s
  baseEjectionTime: 30s
